# A MAGO-
# CABALISTIC
->AND<-
# THEOLOGICAL WORK.
<note>original: "Opus Mago-Cabalisticum et Theologicum"</note>

## On the Origin and Generation
->of<-
# Salt,
## Its Nature and Properties,
->As well as<-
## Its Use and Utility.

Wherein at the same time the generation of all metals and minerals, and all other salts <note>original: "Salien"</note>, is proven from the foundation of nature;

Also, many **theosophical matters** <note>spiritual philosophies concerning the hidden nature of God and the world</note> are interspersed throughout, as the subject matter allows.

Likewise, there is extensive discussion of those creatures invisible to us who dwell within the visible and tangible elements <note>referring to elemental spirits like gnomes or sylphs</note>, as well as concerning Paradise and its location; all of which is presented through the **Magical System** of the Universe <note>original: "Systema Magicum Universi"</note>, by which the lover of truth is guided and led to the highest and most holy mysteries.

->All composed and gathered together by a diligent lover<-
->of eternal truth, whose name is<-

# Preface

## To the Highly-Inclined Reader.

This exceedingly rare writing is shared with the curious world and those eager spirits searching for truth, both in nature and in supernatural things. It would likely never—or at least not so soon—have come to the light of day, had it not fallen into the hands of a person of noble rank through a singular stroke of fate. It happened a few years ago that a stranger came to a famous royal residential city to view it, as well as the court and the surrounding pleasure houses. He lodged there with a merchant. Because the stranger noticed several chemical <note>original: "Chymische," referring to alchemy or early chemistry</note> books in the merchant's possession, he entered into a discourse with him about chemistry. From this conversation, the merchant could perceive the stranger’s great knowledge in such matters. Therefore, curiosity drove him to open the room where the stranger was staying with a master key during his absence. There, he immediately caught sight of this manuscript. Seeing upon opening it that it dealt with chemical matters, he showed it to a friend who lived in a laboratory, who in turn communicated it to a person of noble rank. Since the aforementioned person of noble rank was currently unwell and had to spend the night sleepless, they wished to leaf through this manuscript and return it the next day to the merchant, so it could be delivered back to the stranger, who was staying that night outside the city at a pleasure house.

Now, as the aforementioned person of rank—who is exceedingly experienced in mathematics <note>original: "Mathesi"</note> and the science of nature—encountered quite singular and extraordinary things, the likes of which they had never found in any book of this kind, they decided not to let the manuscript out of their hands until it was copied and carefully collated <note>original: "collationiret," meaning compared for accuracy</note> with the original, in which much that the author <note>original: "Auctor"</note> had considered superfluous, or even too clear, had been crossed out. Whether now—

<summary>This preface recounts the providential discovery of a rare alchemical manuscript, found by a merchant in a stranger's lodgings and subsequently copied by an interested nobleman.</summary>
<keywords>alchemy, chemistry, manuscript history, preface, discovery, secret knowledge, nobleman, merchant</keywords>

# Preface

the merchant was quite dissatisfied with this, and the author <note>original: "Auctor"</note> himself also viewed it with displeasure; yet the power and influence of both were too weak to retrieve the writing <note>original: "Scriptum"</note> from such noble hands so quickly. Even though the aforementioned person of noble rank—who was unable to visit the author due to illness—sent an earnest request for him to come so they might discuss the contents of his writing further, the author refused to agree to this, perhaps out of annoyance that his secret <note>original: "Arcanum"</note> had fallen into foreign hands. Instead, without anyone being able to discover who he was or where he came from, he immediately traveled away with his returned manuscript <note>original: "Manuscripto"</note>.

The curious <note>original: "curieuse," meaning inquisitive or scholarly</note> and attentive reader, especially one not unacquainted with nature and chemical <note>original: "Chymischer," likely referring to alchemy</note> matters, will find with wonder upon reading this writing that this man possessed an altogether uncommon and extraordinary knowledge of nature. He was a true sage <note>original: "Magus"</note> and Kabbalist <note>original: "Cabaliste"</note>, much like the ancient philosophers of old, in that he knew how to use the key to unlock the most hidden things of nature so wisely and successfully.

The content of this entire, curious writing consists of two main parts: namely, nature and supernatural things. Regarding the investigation of nature, true philosophers, naturalists, chemists <note>original: "Chymici"</note>, physicians <note>original: "Medici"</note>, and all curious lovers of true wisdom will find the first origin, the foundation, and the true causes and character of all nature and all things discovered and placed before their eyes as clearly as is possible to reveal to a human being. It is true that the author sometimes breaks off briefly at the greatest mysteries; just when one thinks he is about to open the door to all secret wisdom wide, he closes it quite unexpectedly and leaves the reader in deep reflection. However, to those who understand the character and nature of such things, this is not strange, because it is neither possible nor permitted—nor even useful—for true adepts <note>original: "Adeptis"</note> to bring everything they know to light. Therefore, they are held back from revealing higher things which they may not fully know but can sense. Yet, for those not inexperienced in such matters, much is easily understood regardless, or they are set upon the trail to investigate the matter further—especially if they are skilled in geometry and other mathematics <note>original: "Mathesi"</note>. In these fields, this author demonstrates truly astonishing wisdom, such as no one before him has been found able to produce, having made a cross-section through the entire universe <note>original: "Universum"</note> of heaven and earth. In the figures and diagrams <note>original: "Schematibus"</note> lies great wisdom; for this reason, they have been hand-colored <note>original: "illuminiren"</note> according to the original at no small expense, using colors that represent the true nature of the subject.

His main principle <note>original: "Principium"</note> is Salt, which is of one nature with water and fire, and thus these three distinct things in

# Preface

essence are one, and the Universal Mercury <note>original: "Mercurius Universalis," a term in alchemy for the fundamental spirit or "first matter" of the universe</note> are one; through which the author explains and represents the wonderful divine secret of the Holy Trinity so beautifully that no one before him has done so from the perspective of nature. He establishes the first original source of this Salt as *Shamayim* <note>original Hebrew: שמים</note>, which has its first outflow and origin from GOD: the Heavens, as a pure Salt. This is because this word is composed of *Esh* <note>original Hebrew: אש</note>, Fire, and *Mayim* <note>original Hebrew: מים</note>, Water (both of which constitute the nature of Salt). Now, although a critic or a philologist <note>original: "Criticus und Philologus," referring to language scholars</note> might have much to point out and object to regarding this composition of the Hebrew word *Shamayim* from *Esh* and *Mayim*—especially since the Hebrew language does not favor compound words in common nouns <note>original: "Nominibus appellativis"</note>—learned Christians and Jews are just as reluctant to accept this composition as they are the derivations from *Sham* <note>original Hebrew: שם</note>, meaning "there," and *Mayim*, Water; or from *Sha’ah* <note>original Hebrew: שאה</note>, Astonishment, and *Mayim*, Water (because the waters stand above in an astonishing manner). Instead, following Rabbi Aben-Ezra <note>Abraham ibn Ezra, a famous 12th-century scholar</note>, they would much rather grant its origin to the Arabic root *shama*, meaning "to be high." Nevertheless, our author, from whom one cannot expect such linguistic hair-splitting, had good reason to prefer the composition of *Esh* (Fire) and *Mayim* (Water), as it fits not only his purpose but comes closest to the true nature of the matter itself of which he treats. Furthermore, the ancient Rabbis, who were knowledgeable in the true Kabbalah <note>original: "Cabbala," a mystical Jewish tradition</note>, themselves derived this word from Fire and Water, as the author references on folio 2.

The highly praised and noble person of rank, whose great experience in <term>Mathematics</term> <note>original: "Mathesi," referring here to a blend of math, geometry, and cosmic proportions</note> and Nature was already lauded above, has firmly assured that they have seen the <term>Signature of Salt</term> <note>The "signature" is an alchemical concept suggesting that the physical form of a substance reveals its inner spiritual properties</note>—of which the author makes mention on folio 37—with their own eyes in perfection. Specifically, they found the <term>Cube</term> <note>original: "Cubum"</note>, as seen in Figure Number 15 on the copperplate of the *Opus Magno-Cabbalisticum* <note>A significant kabbalistic-alchemical work</note> marked "dd," as well as the twelve triangular <term>Pyramids</term> as they are described. Likewise, the mystical signature mentioned on folios 38, 39, and 40, and seen at Numbers 16 through 34 on the copperplate of the *Opus Magno-Cabbalisticum* marked "ee," was found to be true. This signature—particularly at Number 24 there, as well as on the plate of the *Opus Magno-Cabbalisticum* marked "bb," Numbers 10 and 11—contains within itself not only the secret of all nature but also the foundation of all mathematics simultaneously. This is certainly wonderful and worthy of careful consideration. From this, one can see from what source the ancients drew all sciences before anything was found recorded in writing: namely, from the Great Book of Nature itself.

From these and other even more clearly perceived true signs, it is easy to judge that everything the author has written in the first three chapters concerning Salts is the pure, clear

<meta>This page continues the preface, specifically following the author’s discussion of the Hebrew etymology of "Shamayim." # Preface

...be the truth. But whoever does not well and rightly grasp these first three chapters—namely, the generation of the salt and the metals, the fiery water and watery fire, as well as the *Magical System of this universe* <note>original Latin: "Systema Magicum hujus universi"</note>, along with the *Kabbalistic characters* and the <term>signature<note>The "doctrine of signatures" is the belief that God marked every object in nature with a physical sign indicating its inner purpose or medicinal use.</note></term> of the salt, the metals, the minerals, the *macrocosm* <note>original: "Macrocosmi," referring to the great world or universe</note> and the *microcosm* <note>original: "Microcosmi," referring to the small world or the human being</note>—will be able to grasp or judge little or nothing of the final three chapters.

On this occasion, the frequently mentioned person of noble rank <note>referring to the noble individual who discovered the manuscript, mentioned on the previous page</note> spoke of a curious *signature* of the salt of the earth which they themselves had found. For when they had the salt entirely purified of its *earthliness* <note>original: "Terrestrität"</note>, it crystallized into nothing but *pyramids*, just like the *Egyptian* ones. The most wonderful part of this was that among them were some which possessed the exact same *proportion* as the *Egyptian pyramids*, of which they have the most *accurate* drawing. Specifically, if one divided one side of the *base* into 500 parts, the height measured exactly 682 parts—just like the largest *Egyptian pyramid*, which has the same number of feet in its layout and height. Indeed, all the steps and the joints of the squared stones were as precisely and well-formed as any artist could ever have made them. Furthermore, this salty, *diaphanous* <note>original: "diaphane," meaning translucent or transparent</note> *pyramid* remained stable and did not attract any damp air. From this, it can be clearly and distinctly seen that the ancient *Egyptians*—who were known to be the greatest and most experienced observers of nature and <term>chymists<note>original: "Chymici." In the 17th and 18th centuries, chemistry and alchemy were often treated as a single discipline.</note></term>, yet were accustomed to hiding their secret knowledge—did not take the *model* for these magnificent *pyramids* from their *imagination*, but from the very foundation of nature itself. After the pyramids were built with their steps, made of pure *marble* and squared stones polished as smooth as mirrors without *cement*, they covered them with a *cement* to make the exterior look entirely smooth, specifically to hide the art and the actual *structure*, but above all the *signature* that was represented by these *pyramids*.

In the Herbal <note>original: "Kräuter-Buch"</note> (to which this *Chemical Writing* <note>original: "Chymische Scriptum"</note> is attached), it is noted throughout that the *quality* of every herb consists in either the fixed or volatile salt, or in the *sulphurous* oil, or in the *spirit*. However, those practiced in *chymistry* know well enough that all of this can be *reduced* to a fixed salt. This is also proven with true and irrefutable reasons in this annexed *Alchemical-Kabbalistic Work* <note>original: "Opere Chymico Cabal."</note>, as mentioned on *folios* 10 and 17, and nearly throughout the first three chapters, especially on *folios* 24 and 34, where the effects of salt and the fact that a fixed *medicine* can be prepared from it are discussed.

We are also certain that all those who make a *profession* of *medicine*, *chymistry*, and natural philosophy, when they read through this *writing* without *prejudice* or harmful preconceived opinions, in the fear of the Lord and with careful consideration, and examine it closely—

# Preface

...[reach] much different and better thoughts in most parts of their profession than they might have had until now, and will no longer so easily attack human bodies with such corrosive and truly harmful medications.

It would indeed be desirable that the <term>author<note>original Latin: "Auctor"</note></term>, who is so highly experienced in the ways of nature, had wished to share more of his great knowledge—granted to him by God—in various parts of this work. However, we acknowledge that he, being a wise person, did not wish to hold back without significant reasons. Thus, we must be satisfied with what he has shared. Since it appears he recorded this only for his *private use* <note>original Latin/German: "privat Nutzen"</note> for his own person and hardly intended to bring it to light during his lifetime, we are deeply indebted to the person of noble rank into whose hands it so wonderfully fell. We owe them our most dutiful thanks for not keeping such an excellent work for themselves—they being the most fit to judge it—but choosing instead to share it with the *public* <note>original Latin: "Publico"</note> for their great benefit. At the same time, because the author himself occasionally gave hope of providing more light and information in the future, we most kindly request that if he still lives and this now-printed work meets his eyes, he might be pleased to provide further explanation according to his kind promise.

Regarding supernatural things, such as <term>theosophy<note>From the Greek 'theos' (god) and 'sophia' (wisdom), referring to a mystical insight into the nature of the Divine.</note></term>, which the author takes occasion to treat in this *writing* <note>original Latin: "Scripto"</note>, one senses from his entire style of writing a mind devoted to God, loving God, and honoring God, the Highest Being, in the deepest devotion. In this, it is very praiseworthy that he zealously maintains the most holy mystery of the highly praised Trinity in God and places the ground of our salvation solely in the merit of Jesus Christ as the only true Savior. However, concerning his particular *principles* <note>original Latin: "Principia"</note> which deviate from common teaching—such as when he extends the merit of *Christ* even to the fallen angels (which Scripture denies in *Hebrews 2:16*, stating that *Christ* took on not the nature of angels, but the seed of *Abraham* <note>original: "Abrahæ"</note>), and his attempt to defend the old opinion of <term>Origen<note>An early Christian theologian (c. 184–253 AD) who suggested 'apocatastasis,' the eventual restoration of all souls, including the fallen ones, to God.</note></term> (which <term>Dr. Petersen<note>Johann Wilhelm Petersen (1649–1727), a prominent radical Pietist theologian who, with his wife Johanna Eleonora, championed the doctrine of universal restoration.</note></term> and his wife recently sought out again, though based on entirely different reasons), claiming that even the damned angels and spirits will be led to salvation after suffering a painful purification—likewise what he writes regarding the spirit-beings in the four elements, ghosts, the souls of men, the "sons of God" who mixed with the "daughters of men," as well as his *concept* of the nature of true faith, the fall of *Lucifer* and his angels, and other such things; we leave these to the responsibility of the author and the understanding...

# Preface

...and submitted to the rational and scriptural judgment of the Christian reader; we only remind the reader that not everything should be rejected outright at first glance. Rather, one should carefully weigh and consider the reasons presented, particularly those drawn from Holy Scripture. In doing so, one will certainly find a rather clear light and good guidance for further reflection in many matters.

However closely we observed while reading through this *writing* <note>original Latin: "Scripti"</note>, we could find not the slightest trace or sign from which one might conclude to which church or community among Christians the <term>author<note>original Latin: "Auctor"</note></term> is devoted. For although he posits a <term>purification of souls<note>original German: "Seelenreinigung"</note></term> after death from the remaining dross of sin and defends the idea that the prayers of pious people living in the world can come to the aid of souls in such a state, his opinion is so structured that one certainly cannot conclude he agrees with the Church that posits <term>Purgatory<note>original German: "Fegfeuer"</note></term>. This is because he rejects as unnecessary and useless those prayers for the souls of the deceased made with the intent of some personal gain or advantage. The author occasionally wanders—as in his opinion regarding the earthly <term>Paradise<note>original German: "Paradieß"</note></term> and its rivers—somewhat far from the literal letter of the text toward mystical interpretations. These are usually not approved by the common <term>interpreters of Scripture<note>original German: "Schrifft-Auslegern"</note></term> in historical narratives, where one must hold fast to the literal meaning.

For our part, we consider the author in matters of religion to be an <term>eclectic<note>original Latin: "Eclecticum"; a philosopher or theologian who does not follow one specific school of thought but selects what they consider the best parts of all systems.</note></term> who did not wish to bind himself to any external community, as can be gathered here and there from his manner of speaking. We also believe that in his harsh words against those who do not agree with his opinion, he sometimes somewhat overstepped the bounds of Christian modesty. According to natural fairness, he should have at least allowed others the same freedom to maintain an opinion according to their own understanding as he himself undertook to defend whatever pleased him; for *one must also use the same law that one establishes for another* <note>original Latin: "quod quis juris in alium statuit, eo & ipsi utendum"</note>. Yet we must praise the author for being—as can be inferred from everything—a man of great knowledge in all sciences and things; therefore, every reader will likely find something that pleases them. We wish from our hearts that everyone may find, for their own benefit, edification, and pleasure, complete *satisfaction* <note>original French/German: "Contentement"</note> in reading through this curious writing, in accordance with the good intention the author has shown in such a work; and we recommend this curious writing to the persistent favor of the highly esteemed reader.

->Frankfurt am Main, the 28th of<-
->March 1719.<-

->S. R.<-

<meta>This page begins the main treatise "On the Origin of Common Salt," following the theological and philosophical preface of the previous page. The author shifts from general religious defense to natural philosophy and alchemical theory, specifically examining the fundamental nature of salt as the first principle of the universe.</meta>

<note>A decorative headpiece featuring two rows of repeating ornamental patterns of stylized foliage or fleur-de-lis motifs.</note>

# ON THE ORIGIN OF COMMON SALT.

### CHAPTER I.

#### §. I.

It is a wonder how many opinions have been produced regarding the origin and first generation of salt <note>original symbol: 🜔</note> (that is, common kitchen salt <note>original symbol: 🜔</note>, which is the true origin of all other salts, by whatever name they may be called). These views come from both the learned and the unlearned; one might almost say there are as many minds or opinions as there are heads concerning this point. In truth, this is not one of the least significant matters regarding the beginnings of natural things; rather, one might almost say it is the first and most eminent in the entire economy of this universe <note>original Latin: "tota œconomia Univerſi hujus"</note>. It is thus well worthy of being weighed with better reflection. One should set aside preconceived opinions <note>original Latin: "præconceptas opiniones"</note> and whatever this or that learned speculator has written most eruditely in his idle chamber—without true practice or thorough investigation through laborious work. Instead, one should strive through true observation, consistent with nature, to investigate its true origin <gloss>of the distillation of the spirit</gloss> and material generation <gloss>of salt</gloss>, as we later grasp and feel it, and enjoy it in food and drink. For judging it simply from this or that <gloss>to distill</gloss> is by no means the way to judge its right origin and essence. Nor is it <margin>There is no true investigation of the origin of the sea [brought] to us...</margin> sufficient to say that it was initially created materially and physically by God the Almighty and kept in the great Ocean as a general treasure chest, and from there led through underground channels or passages <note>original Latin: "per ductus s. meatus ſubterraneos"</note>, as if through veins, through the entire globe of the Earth <note>original Latin: "Globum Terræ"</note>, revealing itself here and there through waves, from which the body of salt <note>original symbol: 🜔</note> is then separated from its water <note>original symbol: 🜔, likely used here for brine/water</note> by fire <note>original symbol: 🜂</note> and presented to us.

<margin>Common [salt] is brought to us from the sea.</margin>
§. 2. It is undeniable among all rational men that salt, as we enjoy it, is brought to us from the great sea through underground passages and crevices, and is separated from its wateriness by boiling; however, this does not prove its true origin in the slightest.

<margin>Concerning its various types.</margin>
§. 3. And so that all foreign opinions on this point may be set aside, and the path opened for the truth from the center of nature, we will first consider salt according to its material form and figure, as it appears before our eyes. We generally have three kinds of it, which are fundamentally only one: spring-salt, rock-salt, and sea-salt. All three are essentially the same in their nature and figure, although they differ slightly in their heterogeneities regarding form <note>original Latin: "heterogeneis quoad formam"</note> when unseparated. A fourth kind could also be added here, and not unfairly: namely, common earth-salt and mother-salt. Yet even this can be purified by a simple manual process so that it becomes perfectly equal in figure, form, and quality to the common spring-salt and the others mentioned. Thus, all three are of one essence, nature, and property. <margin>1. Concerning its Figure.</margin> Its figure is cubic, which is the sign <note>original symbol: ⊕, the alchemical sign for Earth or sometimes Antimony</note> of terrestrial bodies and gravity, to testify that in its passage through the earth, it is mixed with earthly qualities. <margin>2. Its Form.</margin> Regarding its form, it is fundamentally diaphanous <note>transparent</note>, and the more it is separated from foreign matter, the more it partakes of this heavenly, translucent form. <margin>3. 4. Concerning its quality. Letter a.</margin> It is fluid and pourable, so that it may easily pass through all other bodies. Its taste is sour <note>In the 17th century, "acid" and "salty" were often grouped together in chemical theory</note>, and slightly astringent, and it is of a drying nature and property, and yet cooling—even though in its center it is nothing other than an essential fire, as shall be proven in what follows. Thus, we have considered it briefly as it lies before our eyes.

§. 4. We now wish to look around for its right father and mother, which is the primary question here: to investigate its origin and essence more precisely so that we may know it fundamentally.

§. 5. Moses teaches us in the first chapter of Genesis, where he describes the creation of this universe, that in the beginning, Almighty God created the heavens and the earth <note>Hebrew: הַשּׁמַיִם וְאֵת הָאָרֶץ, ha-shamayim ve-et ha-aretz</note>.

<summary>This page introduces an alchemical investigation into the origin of common salt, arguing that while it is harvested from the sea, its true essence is a fundamental, cubic, and fire-centered element established at Creation.</summary>

### CHAPTER I.

->**The Heavens and the Earth** <note>original Hebrew: "Schamajim veeth haarez"</note>,<- which is commonly translated as "Heaven and Earth," fundamentally means the Heavens—that is, the primordial Waters—and the Earth. Here Moses places the Heavens, the spiritual, fiery Waters, first, and this is not without very special cause. For they are the **principle of all things** <note>original Latin: "Principium rerum omnium"</note> or the "principle of principles" <note>original Latin: "principiorum principium"</note> first created by God the Lord, which is almost inscrutable to us **according to its true essence** <note>original Latin: "secundum verum esse suum"</note>. Its wondrous name gives us enough to recognize this, for Moses—or rather the Holy Spirit through Moses—
<margin>What is Schamajim?</margin>
calls this expanse **Schamajim**, which is a composite name <note>original Latin: "Nomen compositum"</note>. It has been described by all the Rabbis (the oldest ones, namely, who were practiced and experienced in the true Cabala) as being composed of **Esh** and **Majim** <note>original Hebrew: אש and מים</note>, Fire and Water. To us, according to the outward letter, this is in truth a quite wondrous and strange mixture. How does it seem possible to mix two such entirely contrary things so harmoniously? Yet this is an eternal truth; only, this fiery Water <note>original symbols: Arige $\nabla$</note> or watery Fire <note>original symbols: $\nabla$rige $\triangle$</note>, without which no creature may live or be preserved, is sought after and recognized by very few with diligence. To describe this now according to its entire sphere is not our present intention; it might yet be found, though such divine mysteries perhaps require a more experienced pen than ours. Yet we do not wish to remain silent about the little that has been shared with us by the **Eternal Light** <note>original Latin: "à Lumine Æterno"</note>, so that we may successfully reach our intended purpose and goal: namely, the primordial generation of common salt <note>original symbol: $\ominus$</note>, whether it be from the sea, a spring, or stone salt <note>original symbol: $\ominus$</note>, or even the wild earth-salt <note>original symbol: $\ominus$</note>. For all other salts, such as all vitriols and alum <note>original symbol: $\text{♁}$</note>, do not belong here, as vitriols and alums are contaminated by metallic particles. Sal ammoniac <note>original symbol: $\ast$</note>, however, is a composite of urinous parts and common salt <note>original symbol: $\ominus$</note>; likewise, silver <note>original symbol: $\unicode{x263D}$/Moon</note> is a composite of
<margin>Letter b.</margin>
volatile acids and fixed alkalines, as shall be proven in the following chapters. The alkalines, however, are closest to our common salt <note>original symbol: $\ominus$</note>, as shall be proven in its proper place.

§. 6. It was mentioned shortly before that the Almighty God in the beginning created the Heavens, **Schamajim**, as the "principle of principles" or "Element of Elements" <note>original Latin: "principium principiorum, seu Elementum Elementorum"</note>,
<margin>Letter c.</margin>
namely the fiery Waters <note>original symbol: $\nabla$</note>. After this, Moses continues and adds **and the earth** <note>original Hebrew: ואת הארץ / veeth haarez</note>, which was the "second secondary quality" <note>original Latin: "secunda qualitas secundaria"</note>. For here, water <note>original symbol: $\nabla$</note> and fire <note>original symbol: $\triangle$</note> are taken in one sense, as their signs and the following discourse show; for the little mark <note>original: $\ast\text{f}^b$</note>
<margin>$\ast\text{f}^b$</margin>
was not yet there—namely, the separation of both kinds (commonly called Elements)—so that the **Schamajim** might immediately find a fixed body or object in which they could manifest and exercise their power. In the 6th and 9th verses, Moses further describes the separation of the two other secondary qualities, fire <note>original symbol: $\triangle$</note> and water <note>original symbol: $\nabla$</note>, from the earth—or rather, the earth from the water <note>original symbol: $\nabla$</note>—and
<margin>Letter d.</margin>
thus water <note>original symbol: $\nabla$</note> was the "first secondary quality" <note>original Latin: "prima qualitas secundaria"</note>, as will be proven hereafter. And the fiery water <note>original symbol: $\nabla$</note> or essential fire <note>original symbol: $\triangle$</note> (as the very lightest thing) would have kept its seat in the **Sphere of Schamajim** <note>original Latin: "Sphæra Schamajim"</note>, but the coarser and
<margin>Mercury <note>original symbol: $\unicode{x263F}$</note></margin>
elemental fire <note>original symbol: $\triangle$</note> took its seat in the **center of the earth** <note>original Latin: "Centro terræ"</note>. Thus, the division or separation of the so-called elements was accomplished from the first principle, **Schamajim**. That everything now—as mentioned shortly before—has its life, essence, and preservation from **Schamajim**, is proven by verse 11 on the third day’s work. There, by Divine command through the power of this fiery water (which has been called **Universal Mercury** <note>original symbol and Latin: $\unicode{x263F}$ Universalis</note> by the very oldest philosophers), the earth brought forth all kinds of grass, herbs, and fruitful trees, etc., before the sun, moon, or any stars were created, for the food and nourishment of all living creatures that were only to be created on the 5th and 6th days. For the Almighty God let His wise order be seen herein, so that the living creatures He still intended to create might also immediately find nourishment and preservation. Only on the 4th day were the sun, moon, and all stars created, as henceforth regular **dispensers** <note>original Latin: "Dispensatores"</note> in this great **World-Economy** <note>The author uses this term to describe the organized management of the natural world and the distribution of divine forces.</note>, to communicate this Divine power, life, and outflow—the **Schamajim**—to every creature according to its required necessity. Now the sun began to use its full power to impregnate and fecundate the fire <note>original symbol: $\triangle$</note> with its atoms (which were nothing but pure fire <note>original symbol: $\triangle$</note>) united with the watery atoms of the moon <note>original symbol: $\unicode{x263D}$</note>. These then further communicated this heavenly seed constantly to the sea, which is filled with it even to the present hour—and not the sea alone, but also the entire **globe of the earth** <note>original Latin: "Globus terræ"</note>. This is proven first not only by the earth-salt <note>original symbol: $\ominus$</note> found everywhere, but also by the dew, frost, rain, and snow, all of which are filled with it. From this general treasure-chest, the sea, it is further
<margin>Letter e.</margin>
guided and led through subterranean passages and clefts through the entire earth <note>original symbol: $\text{♁}$</note> for the preservation and nourishment of all creatures. It was proven above that **Schamajim** is a composite name from **Esh** and **Majim**, Fire and Water.

Now the sun is a coarser part of **Esh**, the heavenly fire <note>original symbol: $\triangle$</note>, just as the moon and all other stars are from **Majim**, the heavenly water. These bodies are still incessantly influenced by the first principle, **Schamajim**. Thus they constantly partake of the Divine power and outflow (from the great river of wonders that rises in Eden—namely, from the great sea of the gentle, still Divine essence—which waters this wonder-garden or Paradise, known to very few people). They now sink this power into the sea through their atoms united in fire <note>original symbol: $\triangle$</note>, from whence it is led through the entire earth for the fertility and preservation of all creatures.

# ON THE ORIGIN OF COMMON SALT.
<note>original Latin: "DE ORIGINE SALIS COMMUNIS"</note>

The closer the sea is to the Ecliptic <note>The path of the sun across the sky; regions near the equator.</note>, the saltier it is, just as experience in those places has taught. In such regions, four pounds of sea-water give nearly more than one pound of good Salt [$\ominus$]. The sun also boils the foaming sea there upon the sandy shores into an abundance of Salt [$\ominus$]. However, the closer the sea is to the Poles, the less Salt [$\ominus$] it yields, as is well known to all Northerners and recognized by the whole world. Little salt is boiled there from sea water [$\nabla$], or at least with little profit; for although the water [$\nabla$] there is also salty, the solar rays [$\odot$] are too oblique, and thus their effect is not as powerful as under the Ecliptic and its neighborhood, where they are diametrical <note>Meaning the sun's rays hit the earth directly at a 90-degree angle.</note>.

§. 8. That the entire atmosphere [$\triangle$] <note>The author uses the symbol for Fire to represent the atmosphere or the "fiery" air.</note> is filled with this delicate, heavenly, Saline Balsam is confirmed by all true Philosophers with irrefutable reasons. They can also, through a certain Magnet or attractive Medium <note>original Latin: "Medium attractivum"</note>, entice and attract this fiery [$\triangle$] water [$\nabla$] or wonderful Salt [$\ominus$] in considerable quantities. Should anyone think that such a Magnet or attractive Medium is itself Saline—which is not the case among those practiced in Nature—let them know that various very simple instruments are known to us. Through these, one can attract in great quantity the salty, heavenly Schamajim-watery-fire [$\nabla$-$\triangle$] or Fire-filled-water [$\triangle$-$\nabla$], which is fundamentally nothing other than Salt [$\ominus$]—namely, fire [$\triangle$] filling the air [$\triangle$]. By a special manual technique, one can separate it from all Mercuriality [$\unicode{x263F}$] <note>Refers to the volatile, unstable parts of a substance.</note> and place this wonderful Salt [$\ominus$] <note>original Latin: "Sal mirabile"</note> before one’s eyes (which is known to so very few, and its high utility to even fewer). Furthermore, many have demonstrated and proven that dew (not to mention rain, snow, and frost, which lack it just as little) is filled with this heavenly Balsam of Life or Salt [$\ominus$], which even the simplest person can experience through basic digestion and further separation.

§. 9. Indeed, when the atmosphere [$\triangle$] is not sufficiently filled with this heavenly Salt [$\ominus$]—caused either by direct Divine providence because of human sins, or by long-withheld sunshine, or by many other accidents—then the atmosphere [$\triangle$] is entirely corrupted and falls into rot. All of Nature must then suffer: humans and livestock perish through infectious pestilential plagues, and even all plants wither. Hunger, sorrow, and general destruction arise, as has been abundantly known from the beginning of time until our day. Conversely, it is also known that during continuous sunshine without any rain, but with heavy dew, there have been the most magnificent, fruitful, and healthy times. The reason [Ratio] is that the atmosphere [$\triangle$] was abundantly filled and impregnated with this Balsam of Life or heavenly Salt [$\ominus$] <note>original Latin: "sal cœlesti"</note>.

For this reason, it always stands in the text: **So it became evening, thereafter it also became morning.** And thus the **essential quality** <note>original: "qualitas Essentialis"</note> of the Darkness or the Night, and its births—namely the Waters—are always the first, though they all originate from the fiery or light Fire [$\triangle$] of the Heavens <note>original: "Schamajim"</note>, as was mentioned in §. 10. Verse 6 relates the **third secondary quality** <note>original: "tertiam qualitatem secundariam"</note> and its creation or **separation**, namely of the Fire [$\triangle$] as the subtler part of the Water [$\nabla$]. Namely, it [the Spirit] produced the sand-making waters, and from these <margin>Lit. s.</margin> at the same time the **Virgin Earth** was produced; from which waters mentioned in this verse, the coarse sand (commonly and generally called "earth") together with all mountains, rocks, cliffs, and subterranean passages [were produced]. Their (namely this coarse earth's) superfluous Water [$\nabla$], however, was gathered alone <margin>Lit. t.</margin> and called the Seas. For he himself <margin>Ps. 136. v. 6.</margin> hath founded it upon the seas, and established it upon the floods.** With this, it would indeed be sufficiently proven, as was said in §. 13, Lit. o., namely that the Water [$\nabla$] is the **primary secondary quality**, and thus what is written in Genesis 7:11 may be very well understood: <margin>the earth spread out upon the water</margin> **On that same day all the fountains of the great deep were broken up, and the windows of heaven were opened.** Beyond this, we could extend ourselves further with other testimonies and clearly show how these fountains of the great deep—through the vengeance of the living God, and often for the sake of man's wickedness—burst forth during unusual storms as a general punishment; but enough of this, and we will now <margin>hath etc.</margin> also prove **from physics** <note>original: "ex Physicis"</note> what was said at the beginning of this section.

§. 14. And we say, accordingly, that all Water [$\nabla$]—one may choose which one they will (we are now speaking of so-called sweet waters)—carries sand with it, or is **potentially** <note>original: "in potentia"</note> able to make sand, even if it were filtered a thousand times. The simple man can prove this thus: let him take rain, well, or river water, put it in a glass vessel, and seal it well, yet so that enough air remains inside, and set it in the sun or in a warm place. He will find that after a certain time, through such constant warmth, this water has gone into a **putrefaction** <note>original: "Faülung," a process of breaking down matter to release its internal qualities</note> and has formed a precipitate. This precipitate is in truth nothing other than a delicate earth or sand; and he who knows the same by its innermost nature and not just **superficially** <note>original: "superficialiter"</note> will <margin>Lit. u.</margin> perhaps see something more. Namely, he would learn to recognize a little of what is said in Genesis 2:7: **And the Lord God had formed man from the dust of the earth** (**Eth Ha-adam Aphar Min Ha-adamah**) <note>The Hebrew text provided in the source</note>. Regarding this mystery, more will follow as far as is permitted. Yet we must mention one thing here that is not unhelpful to our proof: namely, if one deals correctly in **distillation** with this water that has gone into putrefaction, <margin>Lit. w.</margin> one obtains a bright and clear wonderful oil, very fiery and burning, which is sought by few and recognized by even fewer; therefore, we do not wish to speak further of it now. Furthermore, it is known to the **curious** <note>original: "Curiosis," meaning those who study the secrets of nature</note> through sea voyages that when ships here in our Europe load good sweet water for their needs and now come under the **Equator** <note>original: "Eclipticam," here likely referring to the tropical zone or the line of the ecliptic</note> where the solar rays are **diametrical** <note>original: "radii Solares diametral," meaning striking directly at a 90-degree angle</note> and **consequently** most intense, such water then becomes thick, viscous, and as if gluey...

### CHAPTER I.

...when such water [$\nabla$] returns to Europe, it becomes thin again, and after separation, it burns like a brandy; these two experiments are not presented here for fools and simpletons to marvel at, like a cow staring at a new gate, <margin>Lit. w.</margin> but rather to clearly prove that Light (namely that of the Sun) is **something substantial** <note>original: "Essentiale aliquid"</note> which becomes corporeal and physical within the water [$\nabla$], as was mentioned above in Lit. r. §. 13. Just as we are familiar with a certain inexpensive instrument through which, by means of a metal mirror <note>original: "Speculi concavi," a concave mirror used to focus light or heat.</note>, we can concentrate the power or the outpourings of the Moon (in <margin>Lit. x.</margin> which the effluvia of all the stars are concentrated) and present them as a water [$\nabla$]. This process holds more depth than the **slop-cooks** <note>original: "Sudelköche," a derogatory term used by serious alchemists for those who practiced "kitchen chemistry" without understanding the spiritual principles.</note> imagine, for by this means all **lunar diseases** <note>Conditions historically believed to be influenced by the moon, such as epilepsy or certain mental illnesses.</note> can be cured **at the root** <note>original: "radicitus"</note>. Furthermore, it is also clearly proven herewith that Darkness, or the cold Night, is likewise something substantial, as was said in Lit. l. §. 12. And although this water [$\nabla$] is ethereal, one can nevertheless clearly demonstrate through it what was just mentioned: namely, that a power to produce sand and stone resides in all waters. That anyone might imagine they could immediately imitate what is told here to the wise is truly not the case; rather, these things require "washed hands" <note>A metaphor for ritual purity, moral integrity, and proper preparation.</note> and the guidance of Almighty God, which I believe will happen to anyone who possesses a great longing for the source of the main stream in Eden.

It is thus abundantly and sufficiently proven and demonstrated, as was stated in §. 14 up to Lit. r., that all waters [$\nabla$]—whichever one might choose, none excepted—contain within them a sand- and stone-making power.

§. 15. Now we also wish to prove that in the **virgin [earth]** <note>original: "virginea"</note>, the earth generated from the **First Element** <note>original: "primo Elemento"</note>, namely the **quicksand** <note>original: "Triebsand." Welling refers to a primordial, foundational layer of sediment rather than just the hazard known today.</note>—the Region E. F. in the Universal Scheme, Lit. m. §. 12—is the so-called foundation of this inhabited ground, of which it says in Psalm 24, v. 2:
> **For he hath founded it upon the seas, and established it upon the floods.**

Firstly, it is known to all the world that in no place can or may one dig deeper than until the aforementioned quicksand; and those who have looked a little beyond their "mother's borders" know abundantly well that in **Brabant** <note>A historical region in the low countries, now part of Belgium and the Netherlands.</note> and similar landscapes, one cannot dig more than 4, 5, or at most 6 feet deep before reaching the aforementioned foundation of this our earth, namely the quicksand. Likewise, in other places, no well or shaft in the mountains can be sunk deeper, nor can a mountain be dug through more deeply, than down to the aforementioned quicksand. Furthermore, it is abundantly known that in the above-mentioned **provinces of the Netherlands** <note>original: "Niederländische Provinzien"</note>, in various places, one finds very high church towers built solely upon this quicksand, without any other foundation, which have nevertheless stood for several **centuries** <note>original: "Secula"</note> without any wobbling or sinking. Specifically, they lay upon said bare quicksand a layer of such wood or strong planks that hold up well in water [$\nabla$] and do not rot; and upon this wooden ground, they build their intended structure without any other special measures. It is well to observe that one must not dig any wells, ponds, etc., nor throw out the quicksand in hopes of going deeper, within several hundred or even a thousand paces of such buildings; otherwise, said buildings will inevitably lean and sink toward that side because their foundation, namely the quicksand, was taken away from that side. This [method of] building cannot be practiced on any other sand, earth, or marsh; rather, on such [ground], because it is swampy, a foundation must be rammed with long piles and gratings fastened, or helped in some other way. The **reason** <note>original: "Ratio"</note> for the quicksand's excellence and immovable ground consists in this: because water [$\nabla$] and quicksand, as recently mentioned and proven, are very closely related, the latter being an offspring of the former. Thus, they allow no tearing apart or pressing down of bodies of equal or unequal quality; they cannot pass through one another unless one or both are made spiritual. Therefore, bodies of a like quality always maintain a [fixed] state and measure, or one does not outweigh the other **according to force and weight** <note>original: "secundum vim & pondus"</note> in the slightest. And thus it is sufficiently proven what was said at the beginning of this section: namely, that just as these individual buildings, towers, etc., stand firm and entirely immovable upon this foundation or quicksand, so and in exactly the same manner all mountains and rocks, and the entire inhabited surface of the earth, stand upon this very same <margin>Lit. y.</margin> foundation or quicksand as upon their root, from which they were all generated. As the man of understanding can perceive and clearly see in all mountains and rocks: how the quicksand is often found high up under the top surface of the earth, entirely dry in the clay or other soils, and almost snow-white; likewise in the veins of the whole [mountain] or the rock-mass, where it shows itself here and there in the completely solid stone, like marrow in the bones. Furthermore, daily experience teaches us (as further proof of what was already cited above in §. 14 up to Lit. r.) that on the stone cliffs and mountains, the water [$\nabla$] which rises incessantly from Region E. F. (see Lit. m. §. 12 Universal Scheme) constantly transforms itself into various kinds of sand and stone; this can be perfectly seen in various large stone quarries. Indeed, who is not aware that both in the **Harz** <note>A mountain range in northern Germany famous for its mines and folklore.</note> and other various places in Europe, there are fearsome caves in which water [$\nabla$] constantly drips, which **coagulates** <note>Hardens from a liquid to a solid state.</note> in those very caves into a wondrous stone, <margin>Lit. z.</margin> like large pillars and other miraculous shapes—even into the **skeletons** <note>original: "sceletis"</note> of fearsome animals and humans of terrifying and varying sizes—which have been formed and shaped from these waters by the **Air-Spirit** <note>A vital force or "archaeus" within the air that directs the formation of matter according to divine patterns.</note> according to the **Forms and Ideas** with which the entire air is impregnated.

### ON THE ORIGIN OF COMMON SALT.

More regarding this will be said in its proper time in the following sections. Mining histories also testify to us that when miners, using their tools <note>original: "Gezau," a traditional German mining term for the hammer and chisel.</note>, violently break through the most solid mountains and subterranean rocks, or blast them apart with fire [<term>△</term>], they have encountered a water [<term>▽</term>] inside these stones. This water has passed through them here and there, serving as their nourishment and food; indeed, from some types of such stones and minerals, such a water may be produced through distillation. Beyond this, if one examines closely and properly all such stones and pebbles that are grown through with various and differently colored veins, one will truly find that if these veins can be skillfully separated from the stone, nothing remains of them but the bare pieces of the stone in and of itself. One will then certainly find that these pieces of stone—from between which the mentioned veins were removed—will once again fit together as neatly as if nothing foreign had ever been between them, but were merely struck and split apart clumsily; thus their cracks still fit back together. From this, one can clearly perceive that these stones or pebbles must undeniably have contained a water or juice within them, which split and passed through them all at once, stepped forth, and settled between the sides, thus constantly keeping the stone apart while growing and enlarging with it. This is set down here in such detail for the further reflection of the lovers of this art, for truly something special is hidden herein, as will occur again from time to time in the following; and thus what was briefly mentioned in Lit. s. §. 13 is fully proven.

§. 16. We have thus proven and demonstrated with irrefutable grounds: First, that water [<term>▽</term>] is the first secondary quality <note>original: "prima qualitas secundaria," a scholastic term for a property derived from the primary elements.</note>, or the first Material Mother, from which all remaining spiritual bodies, whatever they may be named, are generated and brought forth. Secondly, that the quicksand <note>Welling uses "Triebsand" to refer to a primordial, mobile silica or "shifting sand" that serves as the physical foundation of the earth.</note>, or the Virgin Earth, is the other general Mother, generated solely and alone from the first, and thus can say of itself with full right: "I am the earth made from Heaven." Truly, here is found a matter about which something special could be said, but from which we now deviate for certain reasons. As for what else the quicksand might be, it has been extensively recounted according to its external form: namely, that it consists of the tiniest little bodies <note>original: "ex minutissimis Corpusculis"</note>, which are of a glassy nature. If one observes these through a good microscope, one will find in truth that all of them together are of different figures and shapes, none equal to the other. This cannot be otherwise, because their birth, as previously recounted at length, arose from the water [<term>▽</term>]; each of the tiniest parts of this water individually encloses within itself the powers or outpourings <note>original: "effluvia"</note> of the Sun, the Moon, and all the stars—namely, their seeds. These take on innumerable types and shapes, for every star, as well as the Sun and Moon, was specifically qualified out of the Heavens <note>original: "ex Schamajim," from the Hebrew word for the heavens or the dualistic "fire-water."</note> (as already proven above many times). These different qualities or heavenly seeds, sunk into the water [<term>▽</term>], cause the innumerable different births, especially of stones, rocks, and mountains, and from these the herbs, shrubs, and trees, as is reported in Genesis 1:11 and 12.

This is proven even more irrefutably by the following experiment: if one pours 1,000 <margin>Lit. A.</margin> pounds of well-purified Mercury on a wide field, nicely together and undivided, so that all its parts remain together in one body, one will be able to see and observe the entire horizon and all its parts, and everything located upon it, perfectly within that Mercury as if in a bright mirror. If one then further takes this mercury [<term>☿</term>] and, through the process of mercury-making <note>original: "per Mercuriationem"</note> or in other ways, divides it into an inexpressible number of the tiniest little spheres, one will find through a good magnifying glass or microscope that in each of these little spheres, just as in the previous large body, one can likewise see and observe the entire horizon and all its parts. The same condition applies to the water [<term>▽</term>] and all its most subtle parts, as this experiment with mercury [<term>☿</term>] irrefutably shows: namely, that all, even the most inconceivable parts of the water [<term>▽</term>], just like the whole, contain the "Total Heavens" <note>original: "totum Schamajim"</note> within them, just as it is found and specified in the stars. Likewise, <margin>Lit. B.</margin> every little body of the Virgin Earth, as an offspring of the water, must contain the entire heavenly seed from the Sun, Moon, and Stars <note>original: "ex Sole, Luna & Astris"</note>. It is sufficiently known to those practiced in the true art of assaying <note>original: "vera docimastica arte," the technical skill of testing the purity of metals.</note> that not even a grain of sand is to be found, even if it were only as large as a speck of dust in the sunlight, that does not have within it a little bit of subtle, spiritual Gold [<term>☉</term>] and Silver [<term>☽</term>]. This is then nothing other than the true influence of the Light and the Night, or the Sun, Moon, and Stars. For the first Material Mother is, as briefly mentioned before, the water [<term>▽</term>] originating from the Center of all higher things; and from this comes the other general Mother, the Virgin Earth (quicksand), entirely of one kind with the first. From its most subtle and inner dust (its essential part) <note>original: "Aphar," the Hebrew word for dust or pulverized earth.</note> man was formed—from the living Gold [<term>☉</term>] and Silver [<term>☽</term>] Dust, which also signifies the ever-enduring world. Here is the fence <margin>Lit. C.</margin> that keeps in my all-too-rambling thoughts, which is why I must now break off and revel a little more before I deepen or even fall into the Abyss upon which the Spirit of God hovered (Genesis 1:2). We have, however, satisfied a small part of our promise in §. 14 Lit. r.; by this, the understanding man may elevate his thoughts a little and consider the Microcosm <note>The human being as a reflection of the entire universe.</note> more closely, and perhaps he will learn to perfectly recognize his indestructible and ever-enduring body.

§. 17. Since it has now been fully proven and demonstrated that water [<term>▽</term>] is the first secondary quality...

### CHAPTER I.

...or that it was the first element, and that from it all bodies originated and were contained within it. And then that the Virgin Earth <note>The "Virgin Earth" or *Terra Virginea* in alchemy refers to a pure, primordial earth that has not yet been exhausted by growth or corrupted by external elements.</note>, the shifting sand, be the second element, a secondary quality <note>original: "secunda qualitas secundaria"</note>, being one with the first; from this it is to be recognized how this world and all its created parts consisted in water. Here we must also mention, as if only in passing (yet not in vain or by chance), how from what has been said so far, one may precisely recognize the origin, essence, colors, and content of all ores lying before our eyes today—namely, all pebbles, whose breaking point is the heavens. So now we take up once again the broken thread at Letter m. §. 12 and continue in the description of the Magical System <note>original: "Systematis Magici"</note> conducted there, and we say: F. G. is the circle or region of the subterranean Air [<term>$\mathbb{A}$</term>].

<margin>
Lit. E.
</margin>

In which Air [<term>$\mathbb{A}$</term>] F. G. [which is coarser than the Air [<term>$\mathbb{A}$</term>] C. D., in which the effluvia <note>original: "Effluviæ," referring to the invisible outward flows or radiations from celestial bodies.</note> of the Sun [<term>$\odot$</term>], Moon, and Stars, as shown in Letter h. §. 10, form themselves into bodily little spirits] the essential property <note>original: "Essentialis qualitas"</note> of the cold night—after it has sunk there through its rays—or of the stars and the moon, congeals into the material, sand-making Water [<term>$\nabla$</term>] E. F. Daily experience teaches us that this happens in the upper Air [<term>$\mathbb{A}$</term>] C. D. through rain, snow, frost, dew, and hail; and all those knowledgeable in mining truly know all too well that there is a subterranean air region by the storms in the subterranean mountains and chasms. Indeed, one would think the sorrowful histories of terrifying earthquakes, both in past times and our own, should be testimony enough that this must be so, leaving no doubt about this subterranean air region. Thus, according to the natural order cited above, it must inevitably be there, and so this too has its foundation and correctness. 

We come then to the last, innermost, and central region, namely the subterranean Fire [<term>$\triangle$</term>]. Just as the living, airy fire *Shamajim* <note>original: "Schamajim," from the Hebrew שמים (Heavens). Welling follows the alchemical tradition of interpreting this word as a compound of "Esch" (fire) and "Majim" (water).</note> occupies the uppermost seat of all regions of this universe, so this coarse, earthly fire of this great kitchen has taken the lowest region in the center of the globe of the earth <note>original: "Centro Globi Terræ"</note>. For the region of *Shamajim* A. B. is opposed to the central, material, fiery globe G.; the region B. C. to the subterranean coarse air region F. G.; the upper air region or subtler Air [<term>$\mathbb{A}$</term>] C. D. to the sand- and stone-making waters E. F.; but the seas and the gravel of the earth are common to the aforementioned upper and lower regions as receptacles for their effects. For just as the upper powers unite in the air, as proven above, and are brought down to us through the sunlight or rays of Air [<term>$\mathbb{A}$</term>], so the subterranean Fire [<term>$\triangle$</term>] drives the powers of the stars, radiated into the subterranean Air [<term>$\mathbb{A}$</term>], into the region of the great gathering of waters—namely, the sand-making waters E. F.—and there thoroughly boils them out into bodily little bodies, as shown more extensively above. 

Now, that there is such a subterranean Fire [<term>$\triangle$</term>] no one who knows the primary sources of nature will deny; it is also fully known to all miners. The ages of the world have also taught us this lamentably enough through the many fire-eruptions from the earth; indeed, during earthquakes here and there, this has been observed all too well. And what should a rational person think of the Airy Pool in Japan <note>original: "Arigen Pfuhl," likely referring to reports of Japan's volcanic "hells" or boiling springs (such as those in Beppu or Mount Aso) which were becoming known in Europe via travelers like Engelbert Kaempfer.</note> known to us through Oriental travels? Many more such undeniable examples could be brought forward, but we will simply leave it at what daily experience in the mountains and chasms teaches us: namely, that there is such a subterranean and central Fire [<term>$\triangle$</term>], which shows its powers to those who love the truth almost most of all in the harshest winter. 

For when, with the approach of winter, the Air [<term>$\mathbb{A}$</term>] is filled with many coarse moistures—with which the heavenly seed is everywhere mixed (which was generated throughout the summer during the sun's high position by the moon and stars, but could not all come down in the thin, delicate summer Air [<term>$\mathbb{A}$</term>])—and these moistures then, because of their weight and the withdrawal of the Sun [<term>$\odot$</term>], come down to us frequently at this time of year as rain and snow and are locked into the soil until the sun has withdrawn far enough; and the ground is covered by frost, as if by a strong armor, so that these sunken powers cannot escape again and return into their chaos <note>In alchemy, "Chaos" refers to the primordial, unorganized state of matter or the atmosphere from which elements are drawn.</note> and leave the soil unmoistened; then these heavenly powers are boiled out by the subterranean Fire [<term>$\triangle$</term>] into a viscous Water [<term>$\nabla$</term>] (which specifies and adapts itself according to the nature and property of each seed). This viscous Water [<term>$\nabla$</term>] is further driven upwards by the subterranean fire in the trees and many other plants at the approach of spring. Here we also have to observe the wonderful and wise ordinance of God in the frost in these climates; for if it comes at the right time and lasts until the proper term, we have reason to hope for good and fruitful times for the reasons mentioned above. But if, on the contrary, the Lord visits us with wet, slushy winter times, then this heavenly sap of life is mightily lacking, as it is completely weathered away by the subterranean Fire [<term>$\triangle$</term>] and driven back into its chaos, and nothing remains for us but a coarse material Water [<term>$\nabla$</term>] prone to rot, from the circle D. E. (see Figure Number 1). Through these superfluous moistures prone to rot, all kinds of ailments and diseases are generated for us in the spring and summer. And thus it remains true

<meta>This page continues the discussion of celestial influences on earthly elements, specifically focusing on the generation of Salt through the interaction of Solar and Lunar "seeds." It transitions from physical experiments with seawater and meat preservation to a mystical interpretation of Salt as referenced in the Gospels of Luke and Mark.</meta>

### ON THE ORIGIN OF COMMON SALT.

<margin>Lit. H.</margin>
<margin>Lit. I.</margin>
<margin>Lit. K.</margin>
...that [the stars] are generated, has already been sufficiently proven above; indeed, that the outflows of the Sun, Moon, and stars are *revera* <note>Latin: "in reality."</note> nothing other than the <term>Sun</term> <note>original: "☉," the alchemical symbol for the Sun or Gold.</note> has also been sufficiently proven, and shall be spoken of even more strongly hereafter. However, a small reminder must be inserted here: namely, the closer the seas and all other <term>Waters</term> <note>original: "∇," the alchemical symbol for Water.</note> are to the Poles, the more Lunar they are; and the closer they are to the Ecliptic <note>The "Ecliptic" refers to the circular path of the Sun across the sky; regions near the equator were seen as more "Solar" or masculine.</note>, the more Solar or masculine they are found to be. We prove this through these experiments: first, it is known to all the world that the sea-waters under the Poles are indeed salty, yet they cannot be boiled down—or only with great difficulty—into a proper <term>Salt</term> <note>original: "⊖," the alchemical symbol for Salt.</note> due to a lack of masculine Solar seed. Secondly, if one takes a piece of fresh meat and places it with the skin-side down in a sieve or other perforated vessel, and then sprinkles the cut side of the meat with <term>Salt</term> [⊖], this <term>Salt</term> [⊖] will melt and drip from the meat into a vessel placed beneath it. One continues this until the meat has taken in enough for its conservation; then the salt finally remains lying dry upon the meat, as it were, and will no longer melt. For if one then coagulates the runoff salt-brine again, one will indeed obtain a <term>Salt</term> [⊖] once more, but it will truly be good for nothing further, and one cannot salt the slightest thing with it for its conservation, because its <term>Fiery</term> <note>original: "Δrige," from the symbol "Δ" for Fire.</note> masculine parts have departed and crept into the meat, leaving nothing behind but the feminine watery seed. If this [seed] is again animated by the Sun or by <term>Fire</term> [Δ], it then yields a good salt once more. The reason the <term>Salt</term> [⊖] finally remains lying dry upon the meat after it has absorbed enough for its conservation, and will no longer melt, is that the meat no longer extracts its <term>Fiery</term> [Δ] parts. Thus it is perfectly true, and may be understood, what Christ our Savior says so thoughtfully in Luke 14:34-35:

> **Salt is a good thing; but if the salt has lost its flavor, with what shall it be seasoned? It is fit neither for the land nor for the dunghill, but men throw it out. He who has ears to hear, let him hear.**

<margin>Lit. L.</margin>
Furthermore, mention was made above that the waters taken from our climates onto sea-vessels became thick and muddy under the Ecliptic. But when brought back to these places, they became thin again and burned like brandy; this likewise fundamentally proves what we have just said: namely, that the closer the <term>Waters</term> [∇] are to the Poles, the more Lunar they are and lack the masculine Solar seed, with which, conversely, they are richly impregnated under the Ecliptic. On the other hand, the waters at Batavia <note>Present-day Jakarta, Indonesia; a major colonial hub for the Dutch East India Company.</note> and other well-situated places in the East Indies, loaded for the voyage home, are said to remain good through the Ecliptic all the way to the Fatherland. This, then, powerfully confirms our previous discourse once more: namely, the closer the waters are to the Ecliptic, the more Solar they are, and vice versa.

<margin>Lit. M.</margin>
<margin>Lit. N.</margin>
But before we break off here, we must take up the previously cited passage from Luke once more, and with permission ask: what, after all, does our Savior mean by these words:

> **But if the <term>Salt</term> [⊖] has lost its flavor, with what shall it be seasoned?**

Likewise in Mark 9:50 <note>The text cites verse 49, but the quote corresponds to verse 50.</note>:

> **The <term>Salt</term> [⊖] is good; but if the <term>Salt</term> [⊖] becomes unsalty, with what will you season it? How then can the <term>Salt</term> [⊖] lose its flavor? And what kind of body does it then become? Again, what must that thing be with which one can season it and make it powerful once more?**

Furthermore, it is useful neither for the land nor for the dunghill; what use then is <term>Salt</term> [⊖] to us on the land or in the manure? Since it follows from the words of Christ that the earth is dead if no <term>Salt</term> [⊖] be in it. Whoever knows and understands this mystery, we beg him that he would further keep it silent and secret from the "charcoal-murderers" <note>original: "Kohlen-Mördern," a derogatory term for "puffers" or false alchemists who waste resources through ignorant experimentation.</note> and the destroyers of metals and minerals—indeed, the corrupters of nature. The little that we know of it, we also wish to keep to ourselves. We fear, however, because mention is made of dung and such filth, that such people will take little trouble to fathom these divine mysteries. We will therefore seal these mysteries with the almost unfathomable words of our Savior when He says:

> **Salt is a good thing etc. No one is good but God alone etc.**

For every person must be salted with <term>Fire</term> [Δ]: And every sacrifice is salted with <term>Salt</term> [⊖];

> **have salt in yourselves, and have peace with one another.**

Mark 9:48-49.

§. 19. Thus it remains settled that the essential operation and outflows of both the light and the night—namely of the Sun, Moon, and stars—are nothing other than a <term>Salt</term> [⊖], a <term>Fiery Fire</term> [Δ] or <term>Fiery Water</term> [∇] generated by the Sun as the Father in the stars, and in the Moon as the Mother. Thus the action is in the Sun, which (as we have proven from the beginning until now) works nothing in us but <term>Salt</term> [⊖]. That this <term>Salt</term> [⊖] is an essential <term>Fire</term> [Δ]—indeed, the most wonderful and swiftest thing beyond our reason—we prove with this entirely irrefutable fundamental principle: namely, if one concentrates the essential light (the *Radios Solares* <note>Latin: "Solar rays."</note>) through a good burning-mirror <note>A "burning-mirror" is a parabolic mirror or large lens used to focus sunlight to create intense heat.</note>, one will find all too quickly in how few minutes it passes through the densest bodies, melts them into a salty ash, and finally reduces them to a glass. This operation is truly more worthy of contemplation than many over-clever people may imagine. We have now sufficiently proven and demonstrated to the understanding reader that the essential light, as well as the essential night, are a pure <term>Salt</term> [⊖]. Indeed, even the prime-essential outflow of the living God is nothing other than such [a salt] (known to the true Philosophers and Theosophers, but not to the "slop-cooks" and <term>Salt</term>-cooks [⊖]). Furthermore, all <term>Plants</term> <note>original: "Vegetabilia"</note>—which are nothing other than the offspring of the Sun, Moon, and the stars—testify that this universal <term>Salt</term> [$\ominus$] is a <term>Fire</term> [$\triangle$], as the Holy Scripture thoroughly and irrefutably teaches us in Genesis 1. All these plants can be ignited by a small <term>Fire</term> [$\triangle$] and reduced by <term>Fire</term> [$\triangle$] into their general <term>Chaos</term> <note>In alchemy, "Chaos" refers to the primeval, unformed state of matter.</note>, leaving nothing behind but a tiny amount of fixed Salt. However, those experienced in the art can easily make this salt volatile <term>by way of nature</term> <note>original: "per viam naturæ"</note> and raise it <term>to its first being</term> <note>original: "ad primum esse suum"</note>—namely, to transform it into the <term>nature and form of its origin</term> <note>original: "naturam & formam originis suæ"</note>, which is the universal <term>Salt</term> [$\ominus$]. But enough on this point. It is known to those who have traveled even a little that in those regions where quicksand is most prominent—such as in the Netherlands and neighboring areas where there is much <term>Peat</term> <note>original: "Turff," partially decomposed vegetable matter used as fuel.</note>—this peat is nothing other than an effect of the Sun. Specifically, the rays of the Sun settle as <term>dregs</term> <note>original: "fæces"</note> in <term>Water</term> [$\nabla$] and thus give birth to these <term>Nitrous</term> [$\unicode{x1f73e}$] earths. Although this earth is constantly and diligently dug out, it nevertheless grows back continually, as is well known in those places. And this is not said of peat alone, but is even true of mineral coal (though not in all places), which the unbeliever can fully experience in the <term>land of Liège</term> <note>original: "Lückerlande," a region in modern-day Belgium famous for early coal mining.</note>. However, these "Walloon coals" and their foundation are entirely different from the mineral coal of other countries. Furthermore, curious observers have noted that the Sun concentrates itself in shallow waters (those standing near quicksand with little earth beneath them, which cannot easily seep away). When the <term>Water</term> [$\nabla$] has undergone a putrefaction and finally dries up, brownish <term>dregs</term> ["fæces"] remain on the bottom which burn like <term>Fire</term> [$\triangle$]—to say nothing of many other countless observations that powerfully confirm everything said. This should be enough for the wise, and we have thus fully proven this point in Section 3, Letter A, and Section 11, Letter K.
